- [ ] **Step 3: Migrate `overlay.rs`**

Replace `use machine::Cps1;` with:

```rust
use machine::{Cps1, CpuView};
```

⚠️ `Cps1` stays imported: `draw_sound` still takes one until Task 18.

`executing_pc`:

```rust
/// The address of the instruction about to execute.
///
/// `M68k::pc` after a step points **four bytes past** it, because the core
/// prefetches two words. A breakpoint or a listing keyed on `pc` is therefore one or
/// two instructions late, and for a multi-word instruction it names an address that
/// is not an instruction boundary at all — a breakpoint that "works sometimes".
///
/// Takes the view rather than a board: this is the arithmetic every CPU panel needs
/// and no board is involved in it.
#[must_use]
pub fn executing_pc(v: &CpuView<'_>) -> u32 {
    v.cpu.pc.wrapping_sub(4)
}
```

`draw`:

```rust
/// Draws the enabled panels into an ARGB frame.
///
/// `peek` is how the memory and disassembly panels read the machine. A closure
/// rather than a field on [`CpuView`], because a `&dyn Fn` in the view would make it
/// neither `Copy` nor `Debug` and would hand a vtable to the twenty-odd functions
/// that only want registers. See the memory panel's own doc for why it must be the
/// *side-effect-free* peek and not the CPU's read path.
///
/// ⚠️ `m: &Cps1` is still here for one reason: [`draw_sound`] has not been forked
/// yet. Task 18 replaces that parameter and this note together. Every other panel
/// below reads `v`.
///
/// # Panics
///
/// If `buf` is not a `WIDTH × HEIGHT` frame, as [`draw_text`].
pub fn draw(
    buf: &mut [u32],
    v: &CpuView<'_>,
    peek: &dyn Fn(u32) -> Option<u16>,
    m: &Cps1,
    p: Panels,
    disasm_at: u32,
    mem_at: u32,
    bp: &[u32],
) {
    assert_eq!(buf.len(), WIDTH * HEIGHT, "not a frame");
    if p.regs {
        draw_regs(buf, v);
    }
    if p.disasm {
        draw_disasm(buf, v, peek, disasm_at, bp);
    }
    if p.mem {
        draw_mem(buf, peek, mem_at);
    }
    if p.sound {
        draw_sound(buf, m);
    }
    if p.status {
        draw_status(buf, v);
    }
}
```

⚠️ **`draw_mem` loses its view parameter entirely** — it reads only memory. Do not
keep a `v` it does not use; clippy's `used_underscore_binding` will not catch it but
a reviewer will, and an unused parameter is a claim about a dependency that is not
there.

The four panel functions:

```rust
fn draw_regs(buf: &mut [u32], v: &CpuView<'_>) {
    // …body unchanged, with `m.cpu` → `v.cpu`, `m.total_cycles` → `v.total_cycles`,
    // and `m.board.trace.frames` → `v.trace.frames`.
}

fn draw_disasm(
    buf: &mut [u32],
    v: &CpuView<'_>,
    peek: &dyn Fn(u32) -> Option<u16>,
    at: u32,
    bp: &[u32],
) {
    // …body unchanged, with `m.peek_word(a)` → `peek(a)` and `executing_pc(m)` →
    // `executing_pc(v)`.
}

fn draw_mem(buf: &mut [u32], peek: &dyn Fn(u32) -> Option<u16>, at: u32) {
    // …body unchanged, with `m.peek_word(a)` → `peek(a)`. The `--` branch for `None`
    // stays exactly as it is: see `a_none_from_the_closure_still_prints_dashes`.
}

fn draw_status(buf: &mut [u32], v: &CpuView<'_>) {
    // …body unchanged, with `m.cpu` → `v.cpu`, `m.board.vblank_pending()` →
    // `v.vblank_pending`, and `m.line` → `v.line`.
}
```

⚠️ `draw_status` reads `vblank_pending` as a **field** now, not a call. The value is
identical — [`Machine::cpu_view`] fills it by calling the board's own method — but
the syntax changes, and a mechanical find-and-replace of `m.` → `v.` leaves
`v.vblank_pending()` behind, which does not compile. Good: it fails loudly.

- [ ] **Step 4: Migrate `debug.rs`**

Replace `use machine::Cps1;` with `use machine::CpuView;` — `debug.rs` needs
**no** board type at all after this task, which is the clearest single measure that
the split worked.

Each of the six methods takes `v: &CpuView<'_>` in place of `m: &Cps1`:

```rust
    pub fn update(&mut self, a: &Actions, v: &CpuView<'_>) -> bool {
        // …unchanged, with `executing_pc(m)` → `executing_pc(v)` and
        // `m.cpu.a[7]` → `v.cpu.a[7]`.
    }

    fn scroll(&mut self, v: &CpuView<'_>, forward: bool) { /* executing_pc(v) */ }

    #[must_use]
    pub fn disasm_from(&self, v: &CpuView<'_>) -> u32 {
        self.disasm_at.unwrap_or_else(|| executing_pc(v))
    }

    #[must_use]
    pub fn should_break(&self, v: &CpuView<'_>) -> bool {
        self.stopped_at != Some(v.total_cycles) && self.breakpoints.contains(&executing_pc(v))
    }

    pub fn note_stopped(&mut self, v: &CpuView<'_>) {
        self.stopped_at = Some(v.total_cycles);
    }

    pub fn draw(&self, buf: &mut [u32], v: &CpuView<'_>,
                peek: &dyn Fn(u32) -> Option<u16>, m: &machine::Cps1) {
        if !self.panels.any() {
            return;
        }
        overlay::draw(
            buf,
            v,
            peek,
            m,
            self.panels,
            self.disasm_from(v),
            self.mem_at,
            &self.breakpoints,
        );
    }
```

⚠️ `Debugger::draw` is the **one** method that still names `Cps1`, and only to hand
it through to `draw_sound`. Task 18 deletes that parameter. Every other method in
this file is board-free, and the `use machine::CpuView;` line is what a reviewer
should check: if `Cps1` appears in the import list rather than fully qualified at
this one call, the migration did not go as far as it should.

- [ ] **Step 5: Update `debug.rs`'s and `overlay.rs`'s existing tests**

Both modules' tests build a `Cps1` and pass `&m`. Each call site becomes a view. The
pattern, once per test:

```rust
        // was: dbg.update(&a, &m)
        let mv = machine::Machine::Cps1(Box::new(m));
        dbg.update(&a, &mv.cpu_view());
```

⚠️ **A test that steps the machine between calls cannot hold the `Machine` and the
`Cps1`.** `Machine::Cps1(Box::new(m))` *moves* `m`. Tests that alternate stepping
and drawing must build the `Machine` once and step it through the enum:

```rust
        let mut mv = machine::Machine::Cps1(Box::new(a_machine()));
        mv.step_instruction();
        assert!(dbg.should_break(&mv.cpu_view()));
```

This is the one genuinely fiddly part of the task. Counted: **18 tests in
`debug.rs` and 27 in `overlay.rs`**. All of them are this transformation, and none
of their assertions change. ⚠️ **If an assertion has to change to make a test pass,
stop** — the migration altered behaviour, which it must not. The two files' tests
are the regression net for exactly that.

For the tests that need both a stepping machine and its board (the sound panel's,
which still take `&Cps1`), keep the `Cps1` and build the view from a borrow:

```rust
        // A view over a Cps1 without moving it, for the tests that need both.
        fn view(m: &machine::Cps1) -> machine::CpuView<'_> {
            machine::CpuView {
                cpu: &m.cpu,
                trace: &m.board.trace,
                total_cycles: m.total_cycles,
                line: m.line,
                vblank_pending: m.board.vblank_pending(),
            }
        }
```

⚠️ This helper is `#[cfg(test)]` in `overlay.rs`'s test module and **duplicates
[`Machine::cpu_view`]'s five field assignments.** That duplication is deliberate and
bounded: it exists because `CpuView`'s fields are `pub` (they are — a panel reads
them directly), and because the alternative is moving every test's machine into a
`Machine` even where the test also needs the board. It must not be copied into
non-test code. If a future field is added to `CpuView`, this helper stops compiling,
which is the right failure.

- [ ] **Step 6: Update `loop_.rs`'s call sites**

`run` still takes `&mut Cps1` — the `Machine` conversion is Task 18. So each
debugger call builds a view from the board it already holds. Add near the top of
`loop_.rs`:

```rust
/// A [`CpuView`] over the board this loop is running.
///
/// ⚠️ Temporary, and Task 18 deletes it: once `run` takes a `&mut Machine`, the view
/// comes from [`machine::Machine::cpu_view`] and this duplication goes away. It
/// exists for one task so that the panel migration and the loop's board-generic
/// conversion are two separately reviewable changes.
fn view(m: &Cps1) -> machine::CpuView<'_> {
    machine::CpuView {
        cpu: &m.cpu,
        trace: &m.board.trace,
        total_cycles: m.total_cycles,
        line: m.line,
        vblank_pending: m.board.vblank_pending(),
    }
}
```

and the five call sites become:

```rust
    // was: dbg.update(&a, m)
    dbg.update(&a, &view(m));
    // was: dbg.should_break(m)
    dbg.should_break(&view(m));
    // was: dbg.note_stopped(m)
    dbg.note_stopped(&view(m));
    // was: dbg.draw(&mut buf, m)
    dbg.draw(&mut buf, &view(m), &|a| m.peek_word(a), m);
```

⚠️ **The `peek` closure borrows `m` immutably while `view(m)` also borrows it
immutably** — both fine, two shared borrows. But `dbg.draw` must not be called with
`m` borrowed mutably anywhere in the expression, and the `&mut buf` is a *different*
local, so this compiles. If a future edit makes the loop hold `&mut m` across the
draw, the borrow checker refuses; the fix is to end the mutable borrow before the
draw, never to clone the board.

Expected: PASS, clean. ⚠️ Watch for `clippy::too_many_arguments` on
`overlay::draw`, which now takes **eight**. The threshold is seven. Do **not** bundle
parameters into a struct to dodge it. Add `#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]` with

```rust
// Eight parameters: the frame, the CPU view, the memory peek, the board (for the sound
// panel, which is the one panel that forks per board and is not migrated here), the
// panel flags, two scroll addresses and the breakpoint list. Every one is independent
// — bundling them into a struct would move the same eight names one level down and
// hand the caller a literal to fill in, which is not fewer decisions.
#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
```

⚠️ **The count stays at eight after Task 18**, which replaces this `&Cps1` with a
`&Machine` rather than removing it: the sound panel needs a board to dispatch on. The
comment above is therefore written to survive that change — it explains why eight
parameters is the right shape, not that a later task will reduce it. A comment
promising a reduction that does not come is worse than the lint.

- [ ] **Step 8: Commit**

```bash
cargo fmt --all && cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings && cargo test --workspace
git add crates/frontend/src/debug.rs crates/frontend/src/overlay.rs crates/sfemu/src/loop_.rs
git commit -m "refactor(frontend): the CPU panels read a CpuView, not a board

debug.rs no longer names a board type at all, and four of overlay.rs's
five panels take a CpuView. Nothing about registers, disassembly, memory
or the status line forks per board: both machines hold an M68k and a
Trace, so all of it is one implementation over five borrowed fields.

Memory arrives as a &dyn Fn(u32) -> Option<u16> rather than a field on
CpuView. A closure in the view would make it neither Copy nor Debug and
would hand a vtable to the twenty-odd functions that only want registers;
four signatures gain a parameter instead of all of them. &dyn rather than
impl Fn because the parameter would otherwise propagate through three
call layers for two indirect calls a frame.

The sound panel is deliberately NOT migrated. It is 200 lines reading one
Z80, one YM and an OKI, against SF1's two Z80s, one YM and two MSM5205s —
a redesign, not a rename, and it forks in the next task. So
overlay::draw still takes a &Cps1 alongside the view, and carries an
allow(too_many_arguments) whose comment explains why eight independent
parameters is the right shape. The next task turns that &Cps1 into a
&Machine rather than removing it, so the count stays at eight; the
comment is written not to promise otherwise. Mixing the rename and the
redesign would put both behind one review, where the rename's noise hides
the redesign's decisions.

Three new tests. The executing PC is still four bytes behind the view's
pc, because a prefetching core's pc is not the instruction about to run
and a breakpoint keyed on it fires late. The memory panel reads only
through its closure — asserted by counting the closure's calls, which is
what would catch peek being wired to the wrong board once there are two.
And a None from the closure still prints as dashes rather than FFFF: no
chip here and a chip reading all ones are different facts, and CPS-1's
0x800020 genuinely reads FFFF while being decoded, which is what makes
the distinction real.

No assertion in any pre-existing test changed. That was the constraint:
this task changes what the functions take, not what they draw."
```

### Task 18: The sound panel forks

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/frontend/src/sndpanel.rs` (both boards' sound panels, ~700 lines
  with tests)
- Modify: `crates/frontend/src/overlay.rs` (`draw_sound` and three constants move out;
  `draw`'s CPS-1 parameter becomes `&Machine`)
- Modify: `crates/frontend/src/debug.rs` (`Debugger::draw`'s last parameter)
- Modify: `crates/frontend/src/lib.rs` (`pub mod sndpanel;`)
- Modify: `crates/machine/src/sf1/mod.rs` (three test-only saturation setters)
- Modify: `crates/sfemu/src/loop_.rs` (`run` takes `&mut Machine`; two temporary
  helpers reach the `Cps1` for everything else)
- Modify: `crates/sfemu/src/main.rs` (wraps the machine it builds)
- Test: `crates/frontend/src/sndpanel.rs` (a `mod tests`)

⚠️ **The scope line, and where it is drawn.** This task forks the sound panel and
converts `run`'s signature so the panel can dispatch on the board. It converts nothing
else. The gathering turned up **eight** other `&Cps1`-typed functions the loop
touches — `gfx::update`/`step`/`draw`, `run_frame_to_breakpoint`, `title_for`, `save`,
`load`, `screenshot` — plus `pens_to_argb` and `ppm`, and the graphics-viewer fork
behind three of them, which the File Structure table already reserves
`crates/frontend/src/sf1panels.rs` for. Those are **Task 20's** (the viewer fork) and
**Task 21's** (the loop), and they reach their machine through two temporary helpers
(Step 6) until then. Putting a rename and the
graphics fork behind one review is the mistake Task 17 was split to avoid.

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `machine::{Machine, BoardKind, CpuView}` (Task 16), `machine::Cps1`,
  `machine::Sf1` (Task 15), `Sf1::{fm_z80, fm, adpcm_z80, adpcm}` fields,
  `Sf1::{fm_irqs_raised, adpcm_irqs_raised, fm_nmis_raised, adpcm_nmis_raised,
  mix_clips, z80_cycles, adpcm_z80_cycles}`, `FmBoard::{ym_ref, ym_addr, latch,
  trace, peek_byte}` (Task 12), `Adpcm2Board::{msm, msm_mut, bank, bank_base, latch,
  trace}` (Task 13), `Msm5205::{signal, step, data, in_reset, output, msm_w}` and

**⚠️ Fourteen defects this task's own probing found, recorded so they are not re-derived**

Every one was measured against the real tree, not reasoned about:

1. **`SND_HEAD_ROWS`, `SND_COLS`, `FG`, `HI` and `PAD` are private** in `overlay.rs`
   (`:42`, `:45`, `:51`, `:116`, `:126`); only `SND_X`, `SND_Y`, `SND_DIS_ROWS` and
   `STATUS_Y` are `pub`. And **nothing outside `overlay.rs` reads any of them** —
   grepped. So the constants move rather than being re-exported.
2. **`LINE` and `ADVANCE` live in `font.rs`, not `overlay.rs`** (`font.rs:51`, `:54`,
   both `pub`, with `GLYPH_W` 4 and `GLYPH_H` 6).
3. **A `pub` module's doc may not link a `pub(crate)` item.** Probed:
   `#![deny(rustdoc::private_intra_doc_links)]` (`lib.rs:30`) makes `cargo doc`
   **fail** on `[`crate::overlay::box_at`]` from a `pub mod`. Private neighbours go in
   backticks, not links.
4. **`gfxpanels.rs` — the existing panel module — duplicates `FG`, `HI` and `PAD`
   locally, and its `PAD` is 2 where `overlay.rs`'s is 1** (`gfxpanels.rs:65`, `:68`,
   `:77`). So "the second panel module copies the chrome" is this codebase's existing
   pattern *and* the reason not to follow it: two `PAD`s with different values already
   exist, and a third would be the one that silently misaligns.
5. **`let mut line = |…|` fails the build.** Probed: `unused_mut` under
   `deny(warnings)`. `overlay.rs:369` is `let line = …` because the closure mutates
   only through its `&mut` parameters.
6. **`{:06}` is a minimum width, not a maximum, and a `u32` counter reaches ten
   digits.** Six of the first draft's fifteen rows fit at zero and overflowed in play.
   The fix is not a wider box: it is **`{:010}` for every `u32` and `{:013}` for every
   `u64`**, so a row's width does not depend on its value at all. Measured: the widest
   data row is then **44** and it is constant.
7. **CPS-1's shipped panel has the same latent overflow.** Measured: `CLP {:06} DRP
   {:06} UND {:06}` reaches **44** characters in a 38-column box, and `FET {:09} YM
   {:06} LAT {:06}` reaches 43. ⚠️ It is **cosmetic and pre-existing**: the box is at
   `SND_X` 156 with 36 px of black to its right, so the overflow renders into empty
   screen rather than over a panel. ⚠️ **Do not fix it in this task** — widening
   CPS-1's fields is a change to a shipped panel's appearance, which belongs in its own
   commit, not inside a verbatim move. Recorded here because the next person to read
   `FET {:09}` should know it is nine digits by accident: at ~900k Z80 fetches a
   second, it needs a tenth after **19 minutes** of play.
8. **A 45-column box fits and reaches the screen edge.** Measured: `SND_X` 156 +
   45×`ADVANCE` + 2×`PAD` = **383** of `WIDTH` 384. SF1's box is 45 and CPS-1's stays
   38. With fixed-width fields (defect 6) no row can exceed 44, so the width is
   headroom rather than a bet.
9. **`draw_text` clips and does not panic** (`font.rs:218-245`), so a hypothetical
   overflow is a truncated number. That is why 45 is a comfort margin and not a safety
   requirement.
10. **`Msm5205::data_w` does not arm a capture** (Task 10 Step 3): only `vclk_w`'s
    falling edge sets `pending = CAPTURE_CLOCKS`. A test that calls `data_w` and ticks
    moves nothing. **`msm_w`** is the door — `reset_w`, `data_w`, `vclk_w(true)`,
    `vclk_w(false)` — and `CAPTURE_CLOCKS` is **6**.
11. **`Sf1` has no `set_audio_stats`.** CPS-1's is on `SoundBoard` (`sound.rs:342`),
    which SF1 does not have. So SF1's panel prints **no `DRP`/`UND`** — see the layout
    below. Adding the door is Task 21's, with the loop.
12. **The first draft's row list did not match its own body.** Written as 15 header
    rows; transcribing it and counting gave **13**, so the plan's `SF1_HEAD_ROWS`
    disagreed with the code beside it and `debug_assert_eq!(row, SF1_HEAD_ROWS)` would
    have fired on the first run. The layout below was counted by a test, not by eye —
    and the recount is why `DSC`/`OVR`/`UNM` get a row of their own rather than being
    crammed into three other rows, which is what created the overflow in defect 6.
13. **`CAPTURE_CLOCKS` is in `machine::sf1::msm5205`, not `machine::oki::msm5205`.**
    `oki` is the reused ADPCM *decoder* crate (Task 10 consumes `oki::adpcm::Adpcm`
    verbatim); the MSM5205 wrapper that owns the capture timing is `machine`'s own, in
    `crates/machine/src/sf1/msm5205.rs`. Both paths read plausibly and only one exists.
14. **The counter *fields* are not named after their accessors.** `fm_irqs_raised()`
    reads `self.fm_irqs`; `z80_cycles()` reads `self.fm_total`;
    `adpcm_z80_cycles()` reads `self.adpcm_total` (`sf1/mod.rs:10167-10176`,
    `:10304-10312`). The first draft of `saturate_counters_for_test` assigned
    `self.fm_irqs_raised`, which does not compile. ⚠️ Only `mix_clips` has the same
    name both ways, which is exactly what makes the others easy to get wrong.

**The row budget, measured**

`SND_Y` 90, `LINE` 7, `PAD` 1, `STATUS_Y` 214. The box bottom is
`SND_Y + rows * LINE + 2 * PAD`:

| rows | bottom | fits under `STATUS_Y` |
|---|---|---|
| 16 | 204 | yes |
| **17** | **211** | **yes — the ceiling** |
| 18 | 218 | **no** |

`overlay.rs:120-125` already records this for CPS-1 and
`the_sound_panel_still_fits_below_its_last_row` (`:1417`) already asserts it. **Both
boards spend all 17 rows.** ⚠️ Do not raise `SND_ROWS` and do not move `STATUS_Y`.

SF1 spends them differently: **15 header rows and 2 listing rows** against CPS-1's
11 and 6. Two costs are paid for the second CPU and both are stated in the code
rather than discovered later:

- ⚠️ **The listing drops from six instructions to two.** Enough to see the executing
  instruction and the one after, which is what a listing is for on a panel opened to
  watch a driver run. Do not buy rows back by cutting the ADPCM CPU: a board whose
  voices are silent is diagnosed from the CPU that drives them.
- ⚠️ **The FM Z80's alternate registers are dropped.** CPS-1 shows `AF' BC' DE' HL'`
  and `overlay.rs:397-400` gives the reason — a driver's interrupt handler is where
  `exx` is used, and a panel without them shows a Z80 whose registers appear to
  change for no reason. SF1 cannot afford the row.

**SF1's fifteen header rows, measured at true saturation**

Every field is fixed-width, so a row's length does not depend on its value. Counted and
width-checked by a test (`no_sf1_row_overflows_its_box_with_every_counter_saturated`),
not by eye — the first draft of this layout was written as 15 rows and transcribed to 13:

| # | row at maximum | chars |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | `AF FFFF BC FFFF DE FFFF HL FFFF` | 31 |
| 1 | `IX FFFF IY FFFF SP FFFF PC FFFF` | 31 |
| 2 | `I FF R FF IM2 IFF11 HALT NMI 4294967295` | 39 |
| 3 | `LATCH FF  YM REG FF ST FF IRQ Y` | 31 |
| 4 | `KEYON 01234567` | 14 |
| 5 | `---- ADPCM Z80 ------------------------------` | **45** |
| 6 | `AF FFFF BC FFFF SP FFFF PC FFFF` | 31 |
| 7 | `I FF IM2 IFF11 HALT IRQ 4294967295` | 34 |
| 8 | `LATCH FF BANK 255 @ 3FFFF` | 25 |
| 9 | `M0 S -2048 ST 48 D 0F RST* OUT -16384` | 37 |
| 10 | `M1 S +2047 ST 48 D 0F RST- OUT +16352` | 37 |
| 11 | `CLP 4294967295 MSM 4294967295/4294967295` | 40 |
| 12 | `DSC 4294967295 OVR 4294967295 UNM 4294967295` | **44** |
| 13 | `TSC 1099511627776/1099511627776` | 31 |
| 14 | `FET 4294967295 YM 4294967295` | 28 |
| 15–16 | `>FFFF ` + the widest disassembly (32) | 38 |

⚠️ **Row 12 is the widest data row at 44 and it sizes the box.** Row 5's 45 is the rule,
which is *generated* to span the box exactly (`"---- ADPCM Z80 " + "-".repeat(SF1_COLS
- 15)`) and so cannot overflow by construction. Do not add a field to row 12, and if a
counter's format widens, the saturation test is what catches it.

⚠️ **`{:013}` for the T-states, not `{:012}`.** Measured: `{:012}` overflows after 77.6
hours of emulated Z80 time and `{:013}` after 776. A save state carried across sessions
reaches the first; the second is longer than the hardware existed for.

⚠️ **Two of the non-counter columns were measured wrong the first time**, and both are
in the table above at their corrected widths. `OUT` is `output()`, the *scaled* signal
(`-16_384..=16_352`, five digits and a sign), not the raw `signal()` — `{:+06}`, not
`{:+05}`. And `bank_base()` is masked into a 0x40000 region, so it cannot exceed
`0x3FFFF` — `{:05X}`, not `{:06X}`. Neither is a counter, so neither would have been
caught by saturating the counters; both were caught by rendering the row against the
real accessors' documented ranges.

**Why one file with two functions, not two files**

`sndpanel.rs` holds `draw` (which matches on `Machine`), `draw_cps1` and `draw_sf1`.
The chrome is shared and it is the chrome that must not drift: defect 4 above is a
live example — `gfxpanels.rs` already holds a second `PAD` with a different value.
⚠️ The *content* functions share nothing and must not: not one field on the two boards
has both the same name and the same meaning.

(`:365-570`) transplanted verbatim**, with three mechanical substitutions and nothing

| in `overlay.rs` | in `sndpanel.rs` |
|---|---|

| `SND_HEAD_ROWS` | `CPS1_HEAD_ROWS` (same value, 11) |
| `SND_DIS_ROWS` | `CPS1_DIS_ROWS` (same value, 6) |

Move these six tests from `overlay.rs` into `sndpanel.rs`'s `mod tests`, along with

- `drawing_the_sound_panel_does_not_move_the_counters` (`:1490`)

`Panels { sound: true, .. }` today; in `sndpanel.rs` they call `draw(&mut buf, &m)`
directly with `m` wrapped in `Machine::Cps1(Box::new(…))`. ⚠️ `the_sound_panel_renders_without_panicking`
(`:1529`) stays in `overlay.rs` — it exercises *all* panels through `overlay::draw`,

Run: `cargo test -p frontend && cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`
Expected: PASS. This is the whole of the CPS-1 half, and it must be green before a
line of SF1 code exists — a move that changed behaviour is far cheaper to find now

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the failing SF1 tests**

Add to `sndpanel.rs`'s `mod tests`. The fixtures first:

```rust
    /// A 68000 spin program, for both boards.
    fn prog() -> Vec<u8> {
        let mut rom = vec![0u8; 0x0800];
        rom[0..4].copy_from_slice(&0x00FF_F000u32.to_be_bytes());
        rom[4..8].copy_from_slice(&0x0000_0400u32.to_be_bytes());
        rom[0x400..0x402].copy_from_slice(&0x60FEu16.to_be_bytes());
        rom
    }

    /// CPS-1, wrapped. `a_sound_machine()` is the fixture the moved tests use; this
    /// is it inside a `Machine` for the two tests that compare the boards.
    fn a_cps1_machine() -> Machine {
        Machine::Cps1(Box::new(a_sound_machine()))
    }

    /// SF1, with a **different** Z80 program on each sound CPU.
    ///
    /// ⚠️ The two differ on purpose — `jr -2` at 0x0000 on the FM CPU, a `nop` then
    /// `jr -2` on the ADPCM one — so `the_adpcm_row_reads_the_adpcm_cpu` has two
    /// distinguishable PCs to work with. Identical programs would let that test pass
    /// under a panel that read one CPU twice.
    ///
    /// ⚠️ `machine::sf1::test_video()` is `pub(crate)` (Task 15) so this crate cannot
    /// call it. The five empty regions below are exactly what it passes, and
    /// `Sf1Video::new` is `pub` (Task 7). Empty is legal: Task 4's decoder returns
    /// pen 0 for a ROM it cannot index, and this panel draws no framebuffer pixels.
    fn an_sf1_machine() -> Machine {
        let mut m = machine::Sf1::new(
            &prog(),
            machine::video::sf1::Sf1Video::new(
                Vec::new(),
                Vec::new(),
                Vec::new(),
                Vec::new(),
                Vec::new(),
            ),
            vec![0x18, 0xFE],
            vec![0x00, 0x18, 0xFE],
        );
        m.reset();
        Machine::Sf1(Box::new(m))
    }

    /// The SF1 machine out of its wrapper, for a test that pokes the hardware.
    fn as_sf1(m: &mut Machine) -> &mut machine::Sf1 {
        match m {
            Machine::Sf1(s) => s,
            Machine::Cps1(_) => unreachable!("built by an_sf1_machine"),
        }
    }
```

Then the tests:

```rust
    /// Seventeen rows is the ceiling, and both boards spend all of it.
    ///
    /// ⚠️ **Measured, not chosen.** `SND_Y` 90, `LINE` 7, `PAD` 1, `STATUS_Y` 214, so
    /// 17 rows ends at 211 and 18 ends at 218 — through the status line. This is the
    /// assertion that stops a row being added to either panel without one being taken
    /// away.
    #[test]
    fn seventeen_rows_is_the_ceiling_and_both_boards_use_it() {
        assert_eq!(CPS1_HEAD_ROWS + CPS1_DIS_ROWS, SND_ROWS);
        assert_eq!(SF1_HEAD_ROWS + SF1_DIS_ROWS, SND_ROWS);
        let bottom = SND_Y + SND_ROWS * LINE + 2 * PAD;
        assert_eq!(bottom, 211, "where the box ends");
        assert!(bottom < STATUS_Y, "{bottom} against {STATUS_Y}");
        assert!(
            SND_Y + (SND_ROWS + 1) * LINE + 2 * PAD > STATUS_Y,
            "an eighteenth row would cross the status line"
        );
    }

    /// SF1 pays for its second CPU out of the listing, and the trade balances.
    #[test]
    fn sf1_pays_for_its_second_cpu_out_of_the_listing() {
        assert_eq!(CPS1_DIS_ROWS, 6, "one CPU, six instructions");
        assert_eq!(SF1_DIS_ROWS, 2, "two CPUs, two instructions");
        assert_eq!(
            SF1_HEAD_ROWS - CPS1_HEAD_ROWS,
            CPS1_DIS_ROWS - SF1_DIS_ROWS,
            "every header row SF1 gained came out of the listing"
        );
    }

    /// The rule row spans the box exactly, at whatever `SF1_COLS` becomes.
    ///
    /// ⚠️ The one row on this panel that is 45 characters and not 44 is the rule, and
    /// it is the one row that cannot overflow: it is *generated* from `SF1_COLS`
    /// rather than written as a literal. This test is what keeps it that way — a
    /// future hand-written `"---- ADPCM Z80 ------"` would fit today and be the wrong
    /// length the moment the box changes width, which is a rule that stops short of
    /// the edge or ink outside the box.
    #[test]
    fn the_rule_row_spans_the_box_exactly() {
        let rule = format!("---- ADPCM Z80 {}", "-".repeat(SF1_COLS - 15));
        assert_eq!(rule.len(), SF1_COLS, "{rule}");
        assert!(rule.starts_with("---- ADPCM Z80 "), "the label survives");
        assert!(rule.ends_with('-'), "and it runs to the last column");
    }

    /// Both boxes stay on screen, and SF1's wider one reaches the right edge.
    ///
    /// ⚠️ **The reason SF1's box is 45 columns and sits at the edge.** Its widest
    /// data row — `DSC {:010} OVR {:010} UNM {:010}` — is 44 characters at every
    /// value, so 45 is the first width that holds it. `draw_text` clips rather than
    /// panicking (`font.rs:218`), so an overflow would be a silently truncated
    /// number rather than a crash, and in a 38-column box it would also print over
    /// whatever is to the box's right.
    #[test]
    fn both_boxes_stay_on_screen_and_sf1_reaches_the_edge() {
        assert_eq!(SND_X + CPS1_COLS * ADVANCE + 2 * PAD, 348);
        let sf1_right = SND_X + SF1_COLS * ADVANCE + 2 * PAD;
        assert_eq!(sf1_right, 383, "one pixel of margin");
        assert!(sf1_right <= WIDTH, "{sf1_right} against {WIDTH}");
        assert!(
            SND_X + (SF1_COLS + 1) * ADVANCE + 2 * PAD > WIDTH,
            "and a forty-sixth column would run off the screen"
        );
    }

    /// Every SF1 row fits its box at the widest value it can hold.
    ///
    /// ⚠️ This is the test the first draft of this panel needed and did not have.
    /// That draft formatted counters `{:06}`, which is a *minimum* width and not a
    /// maximum: six of its fifteen rows fit at zero and overflowed at saturation.
    /// The fix was fixed-width fields (`{:010}` per `u32`, `{:013}` per `u64`) so a
    /// row's length cannot depend on its value — and this test is what holds that
    /// property when someone adds the sixteenth field.
    ///
    /// ⚠️ **What this test does not cover.** It saturates the counters, which are the
    /// fields whose width the first draft got wrong. The two non-counter columns whose
    /// widths were *also* wrong — `OUT`, which is `output()` scaled into
    /// `-16_384..=16_352` and needs `{:+06}`, and `@`, which is `bank_base()` masked
    /// into a 0x40000 region and so needs only `{:05X}` — cannot be reached from here:
    /// `output()` follows from an ADPCM stream and `bank_base()` from a bank register.
    /// Both are bounded by their format specifiers instead, and both rows sit 7
    /// characters below the box at those specifiers. The module doc's table is where
    /// they are recorded.
    #[test]
    fn no_sf1_row_overflows_its_box_with_every_counter_saturated() {
        let mut m = an_sf1_machine();
        {
            let s = as_sf1(&mut m);
            // A CPU state that maxes every hex field, and cycle counters at 2^40 —
            // 1.1e12 T-states, 85 hours of emulated Z80 time at 3.579545 MHz, which
            // is 13 digits and so exactly the width the panel is formatted for.
            s.fm_z80.pc = 0xFFFF;
            s.fm_z80.sp = 0xFFFF;
            s.adpcm_z80.pc = 0xFFFF;
            s.saturate_counters_for_test();
        }
        let mut buf = vec![0u32; WIDTH * HEIGHT];
        draw(&mut buf, &m);
        // Nothing drew in the column strip past the box, and nothing below it.
        let right = SND_X + SF1_COLS * ADVANCE + 2 * PAD;
        let bottom = SND_Y + SND_ROWS * LINE + 2 * PAD;
        for y in SND_Y..bottom {
            for x in right..WIDTH {
                assert_eq!(buf[y * WIDTH + x], 0, "ink at ({x},{y}), past {right}");
            }
        }
        for y in bottom..HEIGHT {
            for x in SND_X..WIDTH {
                assert_eq!(buf[y * WIDTH + x], 0, "ink at ({x},{y}), below {bottom}");
            }
        }
    }

    /// Both boards draw something, and neither draws below its box.
    #[test]
    fn both_boards_draw_inside_the_box() {
        for m in [a_cps1_machine(), an_sf1_machine()] {
            let board = m.board();
            let mut buf = vec![0u32; WIDTH * HEIGHT];
            draw(&mut buf, &m);
            let bottom = SND_Y + SND_ROWS * LINE + 2 * PAD;
            for y in bottom..HEIGHT {
                for x in SND_X..WIDTH {
                    assert_eq!(buf[y * WIDTH + x], 0, "{board:?}: ink at ({x},{y})");
                }
            }
            assert!(buf.iter().any(|&p| p != 0), "{board:?}: drew nothing");
        }
    }

    /// The two boards' panels are not the same pixels.
    ///
    /// ⚠️ The failure this catches: `draw` matching on `Machine` with both arms
    /// calling `draw_cps1`. That compiles and shows a plausible panel on the wrong
    /// board.
    #[test]
    fn the_two_panels_are_different() {
        let mut a = vec![0u32; WIDTH * HEIGHT];
        let mut b = vec![0u32; WIDTH * HEIGHT];
        draw(&mut a, &a_cps1_machine());
        draw(&mut b, &an_sf1_machine());
        assert_ne!(a, b, "one board's panel was drawn for both");
    }

    /// The ADPCM CPU's row reads the ADPCM CPU.
    ///
    /// ⚠️ Checked by moving *only* the second Z80. A panel that read `fm_z80` twice
    /// would draw identical pixels, and every other test here would still pass.
    #[test]
    fn the_adpcm_row_reads_the_adpcm_cpu() {
        let mut m = an_sf1_machine();
        let mut before = vec![0u32; WIDTH * HEIGHT];
        draw(&mut before, &m);
        as_sf1(&mut m).adpcm_z80.pc = 0x0123;
        let mut after = vec![0u32; WIDTH * HEIGHT];
        draw(&mut after, &m);
        assert_ne!(before, after, "the ADPCM Z80's PC is not on the panel");
    }

    /// Each MSM5205 row reads its own chip.
    ///
    /// Same argument one level down: a panel that indexed `msm(0)` twice would show a
    /// board where one voice can never be diagnosed.
    ///
    /// ⚠️ `msm_w` is the door, not `data_w`: `data_w` latches a nibble but only
    /// `vclk_w`'s falling edge arms a capture, so `data_w` plus ticks moves nothing.
    /// `msm_w` does reset, data, and both edges — see Task 10.
    #[test]
    fn each_msm_row_reads_its_own_chip() {
        let mut m = an_sf1_machine();
        let mut quiet = vec![0u32; WIDTH * HEIGHT];
        draw(&mut quiet, &m);
        {
            let s = as_sf1(&mut m);
            s.adpcm.msm_mut(1).msm_w(0x0F);
            // ⚠️ `machine::sf1::msm5205`, not `machine::oki::msm5205`. `oki` is the
            // reused ADPCM *decoder* crate; the MSM5205 wrapper with the capture
            // timing is `machine`'s own (Task 10), and `CAPTURE_CLOCKS` is 6.
            for _ in 0..machine::sf1::msm5205::CAPTURE_CLOCKS {
                s.adpcm.msm_mut(1).tick();
            }
            assert_ne!(s.adpcm.msm(1).signal(), 0, "chip 1 moved");
            assert_eq!(s.adpcm.msm(0).signal(), 0, "chip 0 did not");
        }
        let mut moved = vec![0u32; WIDTH * HEIGHT];
        draw(&mut moved, &m);
        assert_ne!(quiet, moved, "chip 1's row does not read chip 1");
    }

    /// Drawing the SF1 panel does not move the machine's counters.
    ///
    /// The same property CPS-1's `drawing_the_sound_panel_does_not_move_the_counters`
    /// asserts, and for the same reason: this panel reads the ADPCM CPU's ROM through
    /// `peek_byte`, and reading it through the Z80 bus instead would add the panel's
    /// own fetches to the `FET` count three rows above it.
    #[test]
    fn drawing_the_sf1_panel_does_not_move_the_counters() {
        let mut m = an_sf1_machine();
        m.run_frame();
        let before = {
            let s = as_sf1(&mut m);
            (s.fm.trace(), s.adpcm.trace(), s.z80_cycles(), s.adpcm_z80_cycles())
        };
        let mut buf = vec![0u32; WIDTH * HEIGHT];
        draw(&mut buf, &m);
        draw(&mut buf, &m);
        let after = {
            let s = as_sf1(&mut m);
            (s.fm.trace(), s.adpcm.trace(), s.z80_cycles(), s.adpcm_z80_cycles())
        };
        assert_eq!(before.0.audiocpu_fetches, after.0.audiocpu_fetches, "FM fetches");
        assert_eq!(before.1.rom_fetches, after.1.rom_fetches, "ADPCM fetches");
        assert_eq!(before.1.bank_fetches, after.1.bank_fetches, "bank fetches");
        assert_eq!(before.2, after.2, "FM T-states");
        assert_eq!(before.3, after.3, "ADPCM T-states");
    }

    /// A listing at the top of the Z80's space wraps rather than panicking.
    ///
    /// ⚠️ The 68000 panel's equivalent case, and the reason it is a test on both
    /// boards: debug builds panic on overflow, and a debugger is most often opened
    /// *because* a PC has gone somewhere it should not be.
    #[test]
    fn an_sf1_listing_at_the_top_of_the_space_wraps() {
        let mut m = an_sf1_machine();
        as_sf1(&mut m).fm_z80.pc = 0xFFFD;
        let mut buf = vec![0u32; WIDTH * HEIGHT];
        draw(&mut buf, &m);
        assert!(buf.iter().any(|&p| p != 0), "it drew");
    }
```

⚠️ **`Sf1::saturate_counters_for_test()` does not exist and this task adds it**, in
`machine`, `#[cfg(test)]`-free and `pub`:

```rust
    /// Sets every instrument counter to its maximum, for a frontend test.
    ///
    /// ⚠️ **Public and not `#[cfg(test)]` on purpose.** `frontend` is a different
    /// crate, so a `#[cfg(test)]` helper here would be invisible to it — the same
    /// wall that puts `sf1::test_video()` out of reach. The alternative is for the
    /// panel's test to set fifteen private fields, which means making them public,
    /// which is a worse trade: this is one door with a name that says what it is for.
    ///
    /// The panel formats every counter to a fixed width, so no row's length depends
    /// on its value — but that is a property, and a property needs a test.
    /// `no_sf1_row_overflows_its_box_with_every_counter_saturated` renders the panel
    /// with every counter at its maximum, and this is how it gets there. The draft
    /// that used `{:06}` — a minimum width, not a maximum — had six rows that fit at
    /// zero and overflowed here.
    pub fn saturate_counters_for_test(&mut self) {
        // ⚠️ The **field** names, which are not the accessor names: `fm_irqs` behind
        // `fm_irqs_raised()`, `fm_total` behind `z80_cycles()`. Writing
        // `self.fm_irqs_raised = …` here does not compile, and the first draft of this
        // helper did exactly that.
        self.fm_irqs = u32::MAX;
        self.adpcm_irqs = u32::MAX;
        self.fm_nmis = u32::MAX;
        self.adpcm_nmis = u32::MAX;
        self.mix_clips = u32::MAX;
        self.fm_total = 1 << 40;
        self.adpcm_total = 1 << 40;
        self.fm.saturate_trace_for_test();
        self.adpcm.saturate_trace_for_test();
    }
```

with a matching setter on each board. Both hold their counters as one `trace` struct
field (`fm.rs`'s `trace: FmTrace`, `adpcm2.rs`'s `trace: Adpcm2Trace`), so both are one
assignment — ⚠️ **not** field-by-field, which would silently miss a counter added later:

```rust
    /// Sets every counter to its maximum, for a frontend panel-width test.
    ///
    /// See [`crate::sf1::Sf1::saturate_counters_for_test`], which is the only caller.
    /// Assigns the whole struct rather than each field, so a counter added to
    /// [`FmTrace`] later is saturated by this without anyone remembering to.
    pub fn saturate_trace_for_test(&mut self) {
        self.trace = FmTrace {
            ym_writes: u32::MAX,
            latch_reads: u32::MAX,
            audiocpu_fetches: u32::MAX,
            rom_writes: u32::MAX,
            port_accesses: u32::MAX,
        };
    }
```

and, on `Adpcm2Board`, the same shape against `Adpcm2Trace` — `msm_writes:
[u32::MAX; CHIPS]`, then `latch_reads`, `bank_writes`, `bank_overruns`, `rom_fetches`,
`bank_fetches`, `writes_discarded` and `unmapped_ports` each `u32::MAX`.
⚠️ Struct literals, not `..Default::default()`: a literal without a field fails the
build, which is the property that makes "a counter added later is saturated too" true.
The panel prints only three of `Adpcm2Trace`'s eight and two of `FmTrace`'s five, but
saturating all of them costs nothing and survives the panel gaining a column.
⚠️ **`1 << 40` for the cycle counters, not `u64::MAX`**: the panel prints them
`{:013}`, and `u64::MAX` is 20 digits, so saturating to it would size the box for a
case the hardware cannot reach. `1 << 40` is 13 digits — the exact width the format
reserves — and is 85 hours of emulated Z80 time at 3.579545 MHz. That is also why the
format is `{:013}` and not `{:012}`: twelve digits run out after 77.6 hours, which a
save state carried across sessions can reach, and thirteen last 776. ⚠️ Do not use
these setters anywhere but a test; they are named so a reviewer can see that at the
call site.

- [ ] **Step 4: Write `sndpanel.rs`**

```rust
//! The sound panel, which is the one panel that genuinely forks per board.
//!
//! # Why this is not one function
//!
//! CPS-1 has one Z80, one YM2151 and an OKI MSM6295 with a pin-7 divisor and a
//! pending-command register. SF1 has **two** Z80s on one crystal, one YM2151, two
//! MSM5205s with a signal, a step index and a reset pin each, and a 256-entry ROM
//! bank register on the second CPU's bus. Not one field on the two boards has both
//! the same name and the same meaning, so the content functions share nothing.
//!
//! What they share is the chrome — `box_at`, the `line` closure, the row and column
//! constants — and that is why both live in one file. `gfxpanels.rs` shows what the
//! alternative costs: it holds its own `FG`, `HI` and `PAD`, and its `PAD` is 2 where
//! `overlay.rs`'s is 1. A third copy would be the one that silently misaligns.
//!
//! # The row budget, measured
//!
//! `SND_Y` is 90, `LINE` is 7, `PAD` is 1, and `STATUS_Y` is 214, so
//! `SND_Y + rows * LINE + 2 * PAD` is 211 at 17 rows and **218 at 18** — through the
//! status line. **17 is the ceiling and both boards spend all of it.** Adding a row
//! to either panel means taking one away, and
//! `seventeen_rows_is_the_ceiling_and_both_boards_use_it` is that fact as a test.
//!
//! SF1 pays for its second CPU out of the listing: 15 header rows and **2**
//! instructions against CPS-1's 11 and 6, which is enough to see the executing
//! instruction and the one after. It also drops the FM Z80's alternate-register row
//! that CPS-1 shows. Both losses are recorded here rather than silently taken.
//!
//! # The two box widths, and why every field is fixed-width
//!
//! CPS-1's box is 38 columns and SF1's is **45**, at the screen's right edge (156 +
//! 45 × 5 + 2 = 383 of 384).
//!
//! ⚠️ **Every counter on the SF1 panel is formatted to a fixed width** — `{:010}` for
//! a `u32`, `{:013}` for a `u64` — so no row's length depends on its value. This is
//! not decoration. `{:06}` is a *minimum* width, not a maximum: the first draft of
//! this panel used it, every row fit inside the box on a fresh machine and in every
//! test, and six of the fifteen rows overflowed once a counter passed 999,999 and
//! started printing ten digits. A fixed-width panel can be measured once; a
//! minimum-width one has to be measured at every value it can hold.
//!
//! At those widths the widest data row is `DSC` / `OVR` / `UNM` at a constant 44
//! characters, which is what 45 columns is for. `draw_text` clips rather than
//! panicking (`font.rs:218`), so an overflow would be a silently truncated number
//! rather than a crash — and, in a box not at the screen edge, ink printed over
//! whatever is to its right.

use crate::font::{draw_text, ADVANCE, LINE};
use crate::overlay::{box_at, FG, HI, PAD, SND_X, SND_Y, STATUS_Y};
use machine::{Machine, Sf1};

/// CPS-1's box width. The cap, not a measured maximum — see `overlay.rs`'s history:
/// the widest text the Z80 disassembler can produce is 32 characters.
pub const CPS1_COLS: usize = 38;
/// SF1's box width: one column wider than its widest *data* row (`DSC`/`OVR`/`UNM`,
/// a constant 44 characters) and one pixel short of the screen edge. The rule row is
/// generated at exactly this width. See this module's documentation.
pub const SF1_COLS: usize = 45;
/// CPS-1's fixed rows above the listing.
pub const CPS1_HEAD_ROWS: usize = 11;
/// CPS-1's listing rows.
pub const CPS1_DIS_ROWS: usize = 6;
/// SF1's fixed rows: five for the FM CPU and its YM, a rule, three for the ADPCM CPU
/// and its bank, two for the MSM5205s, and four counter rows.
pub const SF1_HEAD_ROWS: usize = 15;
/// SF1's listing rows. ⚠️ Two, not six — see this module's row budget.
pub const SF1_DIS_ROWS: usize = 2;
/// The box height, the same on both boards because 17 is the ceiling.
pub const SND_ROWS: usize = CPS1_HEAD_ROWS + CPS1_DIS_ROWS;

/// Draws whichever board's sound panel.
///
/// Matches on [`Machine`] rather than taking an `as_sf1() -> Option<&Sf1>`: an
/// accessor would let this function compile with one arm doing nothing, which is a
/// panel that goes blank on one board with no error. `the_two_panels_are_different`
/// is the test for that; this signature is what makes it hard to get wrong.
///
/// # Panics
///
/// If `buf` is not a `WIDTH × HEIGHT` frame, as [`crate::font::draw_text`].
pub fn draw(buf: &mut [u32], m: &Machine) {
    match m {
        Machine::Cps1(c) => draw_cps1(buf, c),
        Machine::Sf1(s) => draw_sf1(buf, s),
    }
}

/// CPS-1's panel: **moved verbatim from `overlay.rs`'s `draw_sound`.**
///
/// ⚠️ Not rewritten. Every row, format string, colour and comment came across
/// unchanged; only `SND_COLS`/`SND_HEAD_ROWS`/`SND_DIS_ROWS` were renamed to their
/// `CPS1_` forms, at the same values. The panel's existing tests assert its pixels.
fn draw_cps1(buf: &mut [u32], m: &machine::Cps1) {
    // …the whole of overlay.rs:365-570, with the three renames from Step 1's table.
}

/// SF1's panel: two Z80s, one YM, two MSM5205s and a bank.
fn draw_sf1(buf: &mut [u32], m: &Sf1) {
    let (x, y) = box_at(buf, SND_X, SND_Y, SF1_COLS, SND_ROWS);
    let mut row = 0usize;
    // Not `let mut line` — the closure mutates only through its `&mut` parameters, and
    // `unused_mut` is an error here.
    let line = |buf: &mut [u32], row: &mut usize, s: &str, fg: u32| {
        draw_text(buf, x, y + *row * LINE, s, fg);
        *row += 1;
    };

    // ---- The FM Z80, which is where a music problem is diagnosed. ----
    let z = &m.fm_z80;
    line(
        buf,
        &mut row,
        &format!(
            "AF {:04X} BC {:04X} DE {:04X} HL {:04X}",
            z.af(),
            z.bc(),
            z.de(),
            z.hl()
        ),
        FG,
    );
    line(
        buf,
        &mut row,
        &format!(
            "IX {:04X} IY {:04X} SP {:04X} PC {:04X}",
            z.ix, z.iy, z.sp, z.pc
        ),
        HI,
    );
    // `HALT` with `EI` is normal, as on CPS-1: the driver halts waiting for the YM's
    // timer. `NMI` is how many sound commands the 68000 has sent, which is the number
    // that says whether the game is asking for music at all — and it has no other home,
    // because a taken NMI leaves no trace on the CPU once its handler returns.
    line(
        buf,
        &mut row,
        &format!(
            "I {:02X} R {:02X} IM{} IFF{}{} {} NMI {:010}",
            z.i,
            z.r,
            z.im,
            u8::from(z.iff1),
            u8::from(z.iff2),
            if z.halted { "HALT" } else { "RUN " },
            m.fm_nmis_raised()
        ),
        HI,
    );
    // One latch, not CPS-1's two: `soundcmd_w` (`sf.cpp:118-122`) writes a single byte,
    // so there is no second half in flight to show.
    let ym = m.fm.ym_ref();
    line(
        buf,
        &mut row,
        &format!(
            "LATCH {:02X}  YM REG {:02X} ST {:02X} IRQ {}",
            m.fm.latch(),
            m.fm.ym_addr(),
            ym.read_status(),
            if ym.irq() { "Y" } else { "-" }
        ),
        FG,
    );
    // Read off `keyon_live` rather than register 0x08, which is write-only and holds
    // only the last write — a driver keying one channel at a time would show one voice
    // however many were sounding. The same code and the same reason as CPS-1's.
    let keys: String = ym
        .channels
        .iter()
        .enumerate()
        .map(|(c, ch)| {
            if ch.ops.iter().any(|op| op.keyon_live != 0) {
                char::from(b'0' + c as u8)
            } else {
                '.'
            }
        })
        .collect();
    line(buf, &mut row, &format!("KEYON {keys}"), HI);

    // ---- A rule, then the second CPU. ----
    //
    // ⚠️ A whole row spent on a separator, on a panel at its row ceiling. It earns it:
    // the two register blocks are identically formatted, so a reader glancing at the
    // panel while a voice is missing must not read the FM CPU's PC as the ADPCM CPU's.
    // Without the rule the two blocks are indistinguishable.
    //
    // Generated to span the box rather than written as a literal, so it cannot be the
    // row that overflows when someone changes `SF1_COLS`.
    line(
        buf,
        &mut row,
        &format!("---- ADPCM Z80 {}", "-".repeat(SF1_COLS - 15)),
        FG,
    );

    let a = &m.adpcm_z80;
    // Four registers, not eight. This CPU reads a latch, picks a phrase and writes
    // nibbles to two chips; DE and HL are where it does that, but AF/BC/SP/PC are what
    // say whether it is doing it at all, and there is one row.
    line(
        buf,
        &mut row,
        &format!(
            "AF {:04X} BC {:04X} SP {:04X} PC {:04X}",
            a.af(),
            a.bc(),
            a.sp,
            a.pc
        ),
        FG,
    );
    // ⚠️ `IRQ` here is the **8 kHz periodic**, and it is the most diagnostic number on
    // this half of the panel: `set_periodic_int(irq0_line_hold, from_hz(8000))`
    // (`sf.cpp:763`) paces every sample the board plays, so a count not climbing at
    // 8,000 a second is ADPCM at the wrong rate or none at all. This CPU takes no NMI
    // and no YM IRQ, which is why there is no NMI column here and there is one above.
    line(
        buf,
        &mut row,
        &format!(
            "I {:02X} IM{} IFF{}{} {} IRQ {:010}",
            a.i,
            a.im,
            u8::from(a.iff1),
            u8::from(a.iff2),
            if a.halted { "HALT" } else { "RUN " },
            m.adpcm_irqs_raised()
        ),
        HI,
    );
    // The bank, **resolved to the byte offset it actually reads**. `set_entry(data)`
    // takes a full byte against a `REGION_BYTES` (0x40000) region of `BANK_BYTES`
    // (0x8000) windows, so entries alias with period 8 and a register above 7 is a
    // driver bug the hardware would have answered with garbage — `OVR`, two rows down,
    // is how that becomes visible. `{:3}` on the entry so the `@` does not shuffle
    // sideways as the bank changes, and `{:05X}` because `bank_base()` is masked into
    // the region and so cannot exceed 0x3FFFF — five hex digits, not six.
    line(
        buf,
        &mut row,
        &format!(
            "LATCH {:02X} BANK {:3} @ {:05X}",
            m.adpcm.latch(),
            m.adpcm.bank(),
            m.adpcm.bank_base()
        ),
        FG,
    );

    // ---- The two chips, one row each. ----
    //
    // `S` is the 12-bit accumulator, signed, which says a phrase is progressing; `ST`
    // is the step index, which says it is progressing *coherently* — a step stuck at 48
    // is a decoder out of headroom, which sounds like clipping. `D` is the latched
    // nibble and `RST` the reset pin, where `*` is held in reset: a chip in reset with a
    // nonzero latch is a driver that queued a phrase and never released the pin, which
    // is silence with everything else looking right.
    //
    // ⚠️ `OUT` is `{:+06}`, one wider than `S`'s `{:+05}`. `output()` is the *scaled*
    // signal — `(signal & !DAC_MASK) * DAC_TO_I16`, so `-2048..=2044` times 8, which
    // is `-16_384..=16_352` and five digits with the sign. `{:+05}` would print six
    // characters anyway, one past where the column was measured.
    for chip in 0..2 {
        let c = m.adpcm.msm(chip);
        line(
            buf,
            &mut row,
            &format!(
                "M{chip} S {:+05} ST {:02} D {:02X} RST{} OUT {:+06}",
                c.signal(),
                c.step(),
                c.data(),
                if c.in_reset() { '*' } else { '-' },
                c.output()
            ),
            FG,
        );
    }

    // ---- Four counter rows. ----
    //
    // ⚠️ Every counter is `{:010}` for a `u32` and `{:013}` for a `u64`, so a row's
    // width never depends on its value. `{:06}` is a *minimum* width: the first draft of
    // this panel used it, fit inside the box at zero, and overflowed after nineteen
    // minutes of play. Four rows rather than three is what the fixed widths cost, and
    // the listing pays for it.
    //
    // `CLP` is the mix saturating, which on this board is the only place saturation can
    // happen: two MSM5205s at their rails sum to exactly `i16::MIN`, in range, so it
    // takes the YM's share on top to clip. `MSM` is the per-chip write counts.
    let at = m.adpcm.trace();
    line(
        buf,
        &mut row,
        &format!(
            "CLP {:010} MSM {:010}/{:010}",
            m.mix_clips(),
            at.msm_writes[0],
            at.msm_writes[1]
        ),
        FG,
    );
    // The three "the driver is doing something the board cannot honour" counters.
    // `DSC` is `writes_discarded`, which exists because Z80 #2 has **no RAM at all**
    // (`sf.cpp:217-223`, MAME's own comment is `/* Yes, _no_ ram */`) — a driver that
    // assumed a scratchpad shows a climbing number here and nothing else wrong. `OVR`
    // is a bank entry past the region. `UNM` is an unmapped port.
    //
    // ⚠️ **No `DRP`/`UND` here, unlike CPS-1.** Those two are the *host's* ring
    // statistics; they arrive through `SoundBoard::set_audio_stats` and `Sf1` has no
    // equivalent door. Printing two hardcoded zeroes would read as "the host is fine" on
    // a board that has never been asked. Task 21 adds the door with the loop.
    line(
        buf,
        &mut row,
        &format!(
            "DSC {:010} OVR {:010} UNM {:010}",
            at.writes_discarded, at.bank_overruns, at.unmapped_ports
        ),
        FG,
    );
    // Both CPUs' T-states. The whole point of two accumulators is that they can
    // diverge, and a pair not advancing together is a scheduler bug.
    //
    // ⚠️ `{:013}`, not `{:012}`: twelve digits overflow after 77.6 hours of emulated
    // Z80 time, which a save state carried across sessions reaches. Thirteen lasts 776.
    line(
        buf,
        &mut row,
        &format!(
            "TSC {:013}/{:013}",
            m.z80_cycles(),
            m.adpcm_z80_cycles()
        ),
        FG,
    );
    let ft = m.fm.trace();
    line(
        buf,
        &mut row,
        &format!("FET {:010} YM {:010}", ft.audiocpu_fetches, ft.ym_writes),
        FG,
    );

    debug_assert_eq!(
        row, SF1_HEAD_ROWS,
        "the header is what the box was sized for"
    );

    // The listing, two rows, of the **FM** CPU. One of the two had to be chosen and this
    // is the one whose code you read: it has the RAM, it services the YM, and it is what
    // a music problem is traced through. The ADPCM CPU's PC is on its own row above,
    // which is enough to see it advancing.
    //
    // `peek_byte`, not the bus: reading through `z80::Bus` would add this panel's own
    // reads to the `FET` count two rows above, and
    // `drawing_the_sf1_panel_does_not_move_the_counters` is what holds that.
    let mut pc = z.pc;
    for i in 0..SF1_DIS_ROWS {
        let (text, len) = machine::z80::disasm::disasm(|w| m.fm.peek_byte(w), pc);
        let marker = if pc == z.pc { '>' } else { ' ' };
        let fg = if pc == z.pc { HI } else { FG };
        draw_text(
            buf,
            x,
            y + (SF1_HEAD_ROWS + i) * LINE,
            &format!("{marker}{pc:04X} {}", text.as_str()),
            fg,
        );
        // `len` is at least 1, so the listing always advances and cannot loop.
        pc = pc.wrapping_add(len);
    }
}
```

⚠️ **The module doc names `box_at` in backticks, not as a link.** `box_at`, `FG`,
`HI` and `PAD` are `pub(crate)`, and `#![deny(rustdoc::private_intra_doc_links)]`
(`lib.rs:30`) makes `cargo doc` **fail** on `[`crate::overlay::box_at`]` from a `pub`
module — probed. `pub` items like `SND_X` may be linked.

- [ ] **Step 5: `overlay.rs` and `debug.rs` stop naming a board**

`overlay::draw`'s fourth parameter becomes `m: &Machine`, and `Debugger::draw`'s last
does too. After this, ⚠️ **`use machine::Cps1;` is gone from both files** — that is the
measure that this task landed. Task 17 got `debug.rs` to one reference and
`overlay.rs` to one function; this removes both.

`overlay::draw` still has eight parameters — the `&Cps1` becomes a `&Machine`, it is
not removed — so Task 17's `#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]` **stays**. Its
comment needs one word changed, `board` to `machine`:

```rust
// Eight parameters: the frame, the CPU view, the memory peek, the machine (for the
// sound panel, which is the one panel that forks per board), the panel flags, two
// scroll addresses and the breakpoint list. Every one is independent — bundling them
// into a struct would move the same eight names one level down and hand the caller a
// literal to fill in, which is not fewer decisions.
#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
```

⚠️ Task 17's comment was already written to survive this task, so this is a one-word
edit and not a rewrite. If the comment you find instead promises that this task brings
the count under the threshold, Task 17 was implemented from an earlier draft of the
plan — replace it with the above.

`overlay.rs`'s `#[cfg(test)]` `view()` twin from Task 17 stays; its tests now also
wrap their `Cps1` in a `Machine::Cps1(Box::new(…))` for `draw`'s fourth argument.

- [ ] **Step 6: The loop hands the panels a `Machine`**

`run` takes `&mut Machine`. Its eight other `&Cps1`-typed helpers do **not** convert
in this task — `gfx::update`/`step`/`draw`, `run_frame_to_breakpoint`, `title_for`,
`save`, `load`, `screenshot`, plus `pens_to_argb` and `ppm`. They reach their `Cps1`
through two temporary helpers, exactly parallel to the `view()` Task 17 added:

```rust
/// The CPS-1 machine inside a [`Machine`], mutably.
///
/// ⚠️ **Temporary, and Task 21 deletes it.** This task converts `run`'s signature so
/// the panels can dispatch on the board, but the loop's eight other helpers — and the
/// graphics viewer behind three of them — still take `&Cps1`. Task 20 forks the
/// viewer and Task 21 converts the loop; splitting it here would put a rename and the
/// graphics fork behind one review, which is the mistake Task 17 was split to avoid.
///
/// The `unreachable!` is honest rather than defensive: `main.rs` constructs only a
/// `Machine::Cps1` until Task 21 adds the SF1 arm, and a silent `return` here would
/// be a window that opens on nothing.
fn cps1(m: &mut Machine) -> &mut Cps1 {
    match m {
        Machine::Cps1(c) => c,
        Machine::Sf1(_) => unreachable!("main builds only Cps1 until Task 21"),
    }
}

/// The same, shared. See [`cps1`]; Task 21 deletes both.
fn cps1_ref(m: &Machine) -> &Cps1 {
    match m {
        Machine::Cps1(c) => c,
        Machine::Sf1(_) => unreachable!("main builds only Cps1 until Task 21"),
    }
}
```

⚠️ **Call them per use, never bind the result once.** Probed: `step(cps1(m))` on one
line and `panel(m)` on the next both compile, because each reborrow ends at the end of
its statement. A single `let c = cps1(m);` held across the body holds `*m` mutably and
the `&Machine` the panel needs is then rejected — `E0502`. The per-use form is not a
style preference; it is what makes this shape work at all.

So the loop's `m.` sites become, mechanically:

| today | this task |
|---|---|
| `m.board.inputs = a.inputs` | `cps1(m).board.inputs = a.inputs` |
| `m.reset()` | `m.reset()` — `Machine` forwards it (Task 16) |
| `gfx.update(&a, m)` | `gfx.update(&a, cps1_ref(m))` |
| `m.video.enable = gfx.mask()` | `cps1(m).video.enable = gfx.mask()` |
| `m.step_instruction()` | `m.step_instruction()` — forwarded |
| `m.drain_samples()` | `m.drain_samples()` — forwarded |
| `m.sound.set_audio_stats(…)` | `cps1(m).sound.set_audio_stats(…)` |
| `m.render()` | `m.render()` — forwarded |
| `pens_to_argb(&m.video, …)` | `pens_to_argb(&cps1_ref(m).video, …)` |
| `gfx.draw(&mut buf, m)` | `gfx.draw(&mut buf, cps1_ref(m))` |
| `run_frame_to_breakpoint(m, dbg)` | `run_frame_to_breakpoint(cps1(m), dbg)` |
| `title_for(&s, m, …)` | `title_for(&s, cps1_ref(m), …)` |
| `save(m, o, &mut s)` / `load` / `screenshot` | `…(cps1_ref(m), …)` / `cps1(m)` for `load` |
| `dbg.draw(&mut buf, &view(m), …, m)` | `dbg.draw(&mut buf, &view(cps1_ref(m)), …, m)` |

⚠️ **`view()` — Task 17's temporary — keeps taking `&Cps1` and is now fed
`cps1_ref(m)`.** It could take `&Machine` and call `cpu_view()`, and Task 21 deletes it
either way; leaving it alone keeps this task's `loop_.rs` diff to argument sites only.

`main.rs` wraps the machine it builds:

```rust
    // ⚠️ One arm until Task 21 adds the SF1 driver's, at which point the board comes
    // from the ROM set rather than a flag — a user who has sf2.zip should not have to
    // tell the emulator what sf2.zip is.
    let mut machine = Machine::Cps1(Box::new(build_cps1(&regions)?));
```

⚠️ **`const BOARD` (`loop_.rs:92`) stays a constant this task.** Its own doc says it
becomes a `LoopOpts` field "when the SF1 driver lands" — which is Task 21, by which
point Task 19 has made `BOARD_SF1` and `Sf1State` exist. A field with one possible
value, set from a `match` with one arm, is scaffolding that reads as a finished
decision.

⚠️ **`FramePacer::cps1()` (`loop_.rs:124`) also stays.** `FramePacer::new(m.frame_ns(),
MAX_CATCH_UP)` is the eventual form and `new` is already `const` and public
(`pace.rs:57`), but on a loop that can only hold a CPS-1 it computes the same
16,768,000 ns by a longer route. Task 21 changes it in the commit that makes the other
board reachable.

- [ ] **Step 8: Commit**

```bash
cargo fmt --all && cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings && cargo test --workspace && cargo doc --no-deps --workspace
git add crates/frontend/src/sndpanel.rs crates/frontend/src/{lib,overlay}.rs crates/machine/src/sf1/
git commit -m "feat(frontend): fork the sound panel per board

The one panel that genuinely differs. CPS-1 has one Z80, one YM2151 and an
OKI with a pin-7 divisor and a pending command; SF1 has two Z80s on one
crystal, one YM, two MSM5205s with a signal, step index and reset pin each,
and a 256-entry bank register on the second CPU's bus. Not one field on the
two boards has both the same name and the same meaning, so the content
functions share nothing — but the chrome does, which is why both live in one
file. gfxpanels.rs shows the alternative's cost: it holds its own FG, HI and
PAD, and its PAD is 2 where overlay.rs's is 1.

CPS-1's panel is a verbatim move: same rows, same format strings, same
comments, three renames to CPS1_ forms at identical values. Its six existing
tests moved with it and pass unchanged.

The row budget was measured, not chosen. SND_Y is 90, LINE is 7, PAD is 1
and STATUS_Y is 214, so 17 rows ends at 211 and 18 ends at 218 — through the
status line. Both boards spend all 17. SF1 pays for its second CPU out of
the listing: 15 header rows and 2 instructions against CPS-1's 11 and 6, and
it drops the FM Z80's alternate-register row. Both losses are in the code
rather than silently taken. A row goes to a '---- ADPCM Z80 ----' rule,
which earns it: the two register blocks are identically formatted, and a
reader chasing a missing voice must not read one CPU's PC as the other's.
The rule is generated to span the box rather than written as a literal, so
it cannot be the row that overflows when SF1_COLS changes.

Every field on the SF1 panel is fixed-width — {:010} per u32, {:013} per
u64 — so no row's length depends on its value, and its box is 45 columns
where CPS-1's is 38, one pixel short of the screen edge. Neither is
cosmetic. {:06} is a *minimum* width, not a maximum: the first draft
formatted counters that way, fit inside the box on a fresh machine and in
every test, and six of its fifteen rows overflowed once a counter passed
999,999 and began printing ten digits. Fixed widths make the panel
measurable once instead of at every value it can hold; at them, the widest
row is DSC/OVR/UNM at a constant 44, which is what 45 columns is for.
draw_text clips rather than panicking, so the consequence would have been a
silently truncated number — and, in a box not at the screen edge, ink over
whatever is to its right.
no_sf1_row_overflows_its_box_with_every_counter_saturated renders with every
counter maxed, which is the only way to see it.

Two non-counter columns were measured wrong too, and no counter test would
have caught either. OUT is output(), the *scaled* signal — (signal &
!DAC_MASK) * DAC_TO_I16, so -16_384..=16_352, five digits and a sign — and
needs {:+06}, not S's {:+05}. The bank's byte offset is masked into a
0x40000 region and so cannot exceed 0x3FFFF, needing {:05X} rather than
{:06X}. Both were found by rendering the row against the accessors'
documented ranges; both are recorded in the module doc's table of measured
widths, which is where the next person adding a field should look.

The numbers were chosen for what they diagnose. The 8 kHz periodic IRQ
count, because sf.cpp:763's set_periodic_int paces every sample the board
plays and a count not climbing at 8,000 a second is ADPCM at the wrong rate
or none. The unmapped-port count, on its own row with the two other
'the driver asked for something the board cannot honour' numbers. The bank resolved to its byte offset plus the overrun count,
because set_entry takes a full byte against a region holding eight windows.
Each chip's step index, because a step stuck at 48 is a decoder out of
headroom, which sounds like clipping. Each reset pin, because a chip in
reset with a nonzero latch is silence with everything else looking right.
And writes_discarded, because Z80 #2 has no RAM at all — MAME's own comment
is /* Yes, _no_ ram */ — so a driver assuming a scratchpad shows a climbing
number here and nothing else wrong.

SF1 prints no DRP/UND where CPS-1 does. Those are the host's ring
statistics, they arrive through SoundBoard::set_audio_stats, and Sf1 has no
equivalent door yet. Two hardcoded zeroes would read as 'the host is fine'
on a board that has never been asked; Task 21 adds the door with the loop.

overlay.rs and debug.rs now name no board type: Task 17 got debug.rs to one
reference and overlay.rs to one function, and this removes both. run takes a
&mut Machine so the panel can dispatch on the board, but the loop's eight
other &Cps1 helpers — and the graphics viewer behind three of them — reach
their machine through two temporary per-use helpers. Per use, not one
binding: a single `let c = cps1(m)` holds *m mutably for the body and the
&Machine the panel needs is then rejected, E0502. Task 20 forks the graphics
viewers and Task 21 converts those eight together with the SF1 pixel path,
where one signature change makes all of it consistent in one review.

const BOARD and FramePacer::cps1() also stay. Both become board-derived in
Task 21; on a loop that can only hold a CPS-1 they would be a field with one
possible value and a longer route to the same 16,768,000 ns — scaffolding
that reads as a finished decision."
```
